{"parentUuid":"b79b8f14-b676-49e4-9aab-ab1cadae46cd","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aec4ab0b760cd2873","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- crucible: How to operate CRUCIBLE — the authorized, prove-don't-guess offensive-security engine in framework/v2. Attack from disjoint lenses, refute each objection with a real repro, report only what survives. - Before merging any AEGIS/FORGE domain stream, alongside (not instead of) the red-pen agent. Pass the domain spec via args. On the email-auth stream it caught 3 CRITICAL/LOW defects PAST a RED-PEN PASS — near-zero-FP cannot be self-certified by one reviewer.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. {"parentUuid":"be4381eb-8210-40f7-993f-c1bca2f5c197","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"aec4ab0b760cd2873","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0132Axvgr2KVDicWTfiDhYfP","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# SIGIL spine bounded-growth — DESIGN BRIEF (distilled from the understand phase)\n2\t\n3\tThe SIGIL spine is an append-only, hash-chained, Ed25519-signed JSONL log at `~/.sigil/spine/spine.jsonl`\n4\t(93 MB / 43,412 records, growing). GOAL: bound its growth / per-file size / read latency **without\n5\tweakening tamper-evidence or the append-only guarantee**. 34 consumers across 464 call sites read it;\n6\t23 do FULL scans; 9 need old-seq resolution. Full raw analysis (invariants, per-consumer contract,\n7\ttamper-conflict, durability, critique) is in `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/tasks/w705miq65.output` — read it for detail. Code is at `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/spine/*` and `sigil/reuse/{chain,canonical,models}.py`.\n8\t\n9\t## The 19 invariants a design MUST preserve (condensed)\n10\t\n11\t1. **chain-anchored-from-genesis** — `verify_chain` seeds `prev=_GENESIS_PREV` and rejects entries[0] if\n12\t   its prev_hash != genesis. (It does NOT require entries[0].seq==0 — only prev==genesis + contiguity —\n13\t   so a rebased chain at seq K is valid IFF K's prev_hash is rewritten to a genesis-equivalent value.)\n14\t2. **strict-seq-contiguity** — seq[i]==seq[i-1]+1, no gaps/dupes. Prune may only remove a CONTIGUOUS\n15\t   PREFIX, never punch interior holes.\n16\t3. **seq-is-immutable-primary-key** — never renumber; entry_hash binds seq; parent_id/supersedes_id/\n17\t   cursors/graph anchors reference seq. Keep original seqs across any compaction.\n18\t4. **signed-head-entry_count-is-the-anti-truncation-gate** — `classify_head` flags `len(entries) <\n19\t   head.entry_count` as TAMPERING; entry_count is ABSOLUTE. THE central conflict: prune ⇒ shorter ⇒ reads\n20\t   as rollback. Any prune MUST re-sign a snapshot-aware head atomically.\n21\t5. **monotonic-last_seq anti-rollback** — tip seq never decreases (verify_head prev_highwater [dormant on\n22\t   checkpoint path]; SpineTailer._high_water in-memory). Front-prune keeps tip seq ⇒ safe; renumber ⇒\n23\t   false trip + real-truncation-indistinguishable.\n24\t6. **append-only / offsets-never-move / no-in-place-rewrite** — never rewrite in place; build-then-\n25\t   os.replace. In-place same-or-larger rewrite is UNDETECTED by the size-only index heuristic ⇒ silent\n26\t   wrong reads.\n27\t7. **torn-tail-truncate-before-append (BLOCK-1)** — must run on the ACTIVE append segment only; a sealed\n28\t   segment that ends torn will never be truncated by a future append ⇒ permanent false verify() gap\n29\t   (critique A2: rotation must run `_last_valid_boundary` truncate at SEAL time).\n30\t8. **fsync durability** — acked append fsync'd; head swapped via temp+fsync+os.replace+dir-fsync\n31\t   (`checkpoint._atomic_write_text`). Every rotation output must match this.\n32\t9. **index extend/rebuild correctness under external mutation** — detection is SIZE-ONLY today; must\n33\t   become IDENTITY/EPOCH-based (st_dev/st_ino + manifest generation), and segment-aware (key by\n34\t   (segment,offset)). Rotation must not read as an in-place shrink (which is a truncation attack).\n35\t10. **replay-stable content digest (ts excluded)** — any snapshot/manifest record must digest via the\n36\t    same `append()` content shape + canonical_json (ts excluded) or its binding check fails.\n37\t11. **two-layer unkeyed integrity vs the keyed signed head** — a compactor is a recompute-capable writer;\n38\t    verify() alone can't certify a compaction. The owner-signed head is the authority; compaction must be\n39\t    owner-key-gated.\n40\t12. **read-path presents ONE contiguous genesis chain** — entries()/iter_records(-1)/verify()/count()\n41\t    must transparently concatenate snapshot-front + sealed segments + active segment in seq order, no\n42\t    gap/dup at a seam, so all 34 consumers keep working unchanged.\n43\t13. **durable tip determines next_seq (no seq reuse)** — `_read_last_entry`/next_seq must resolve the tip\n44\t    from the ACTIVE segment (true global max seq).\n45\t14. **cross-writer serialization must cover the compactor** — spine_lock (RLock) + flock. CRITICAL: flock\n46\t    binds to the INODE, not the path; os.replace swaps the inode, so flock-on-target CANNOT exclude a\n47\t    cross-process appender. Need a PATH-STABLE lockfile (`spine.lock`) both append & compaction flock\n48\t    before resolving the active segment.\n49\t15. **monotonic-state-by-full-scan must survive** — per-device nonce high-water (envelope.py), newest\n50\t    warden_checkpoint per pubkey (cli.py), account.create cap count (actor_scope/actor), device\n51\t    authorize/revoke ledger (mesh/registry), kill-switch latch (killswitch), promotion grants\n52\t    (promotion), unresolved queued approvals (approvals), archivist supersession + promotion-key ledger\n53\t    (revise), open threads/commitments/contradictions + citations (queries.py). Rotation (retain-all)\n54\t    keeps these IF scans span segments; PRUNE would silently corrupt them unless each is folded into the\n55\t    signed snapshot.\n56\t16. **referential integrity of parent_id/supersedes_id across a prune** — never dangle; never mutate a\n57\t    retained record's parent/supersedes (breaks its binding).\n58\t17. **canonical/domain primitives frozen** — snapshot/head schema changes need a schema_version bump +\n59\t    migration, computed by the same reuse functions.\n60\t18. **single signed head is the commit point** — extend to carry a snapshot base coherently; keep ONE\n61\t    atomically-published head.\n62\t19. **genesis-vs-empty count semantics** — a compacted store is never \"empty\"; next_seq/last_seq must\n63\t    derive from the snapshot base, not physical len==0.\n64\t\n65\t## Critique additions (must be in any design)\n66\t- **A1 closed head schema** — `SignedChainHead`/`ChainEntry` are `extra=\"forbid\"`. A v2 head with snapshot\n67\t  fields is a BREAKING wire migration; today the two head-consumers fail DIFFERENTLY (SpineTailer swallows\n68\t  ⇒ silent un-anchored; verify_checkpoint has no try/except ⇒ crashes `sigil verify` + mcp ingest_status).\n69\t- **A3 next_seq is cached** — a long-lived store instance compacted by another process keeps stale\n70\t  next_seq; must invalidate on epoch change.\n71\t- **A4 size-as-change-token in THREE places** — store._ensure_index, killswitch.py:57, gesture/session.py:121.\n72\t  A same-size rotation serves a STALE kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction. Fix must be UNIFIED\n73\t  (shared epoch token).\n74\t- **Missing consumers** — consolidate/queries.py (full-scan, citation-heavy MCP surface — first-class),\n75\t  consolidate/gate.py (get(seq) grounding), ingest/transcript.py (sets parent_id ⇒ referential integrity).\n76\t- **C1 (decisive)** — NO durable anti-rollback floor lives OUTSIDE the compactable data. Hard-prune turns\n77\t  the `n<signed_n` truncation alarm into a no-op for the pruned range, so a cold/fresh verifier can't\n78\t  distinguish a legit compaction-baseline from a replayed old (short-spine, low-count-head) pair. Closing\n79\t  this needs a durable external floor (per-verifier local last-accepted last_seq, and/or authenticated\n80\t  head delivery a mirror can't roll back). WARM same-host verifiers are fine; COLD/fresh are not.\n81\t- **C2** — once classify_head slices only the LIVE window, the pruned prefix is NO LONGER re-hashed, so the\n82\t  snapshot's committed digest MUST be a Merkle/hash commitment binding EVERY pruned record (else a pre-\n83\t  archival edit is unprovable). This is an invariant, not a nice-to-have.\n84\t- **D1** — must NOT hold the append lock across the O(file) heavy copy (would block a kill-switch PANIC\n85\t  append). Heavy copy of immutable records is lock-free; only a bounded seal→publish critical section\n86\t  takes the lock.\n87\t- **D2** — live-reader vs unlink race across the manifest indirection: readers must snapshot ALL segment\n88\t  fds right after reading the manifest; unlink deferred behind a reader grace/refcount.\n89\t\n90\t## Cutover protocol (durability analysis — the required shape)\n91\tONE authoritative **manifest** naming the active segment set = the sole linearization point, swapped via\n92\t`_atomic_write_text` (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). Ordering: write+fsync new segment(s) → write+fsync\n93\tnew manifest → os.replace manifest (the atomic instant) → dir-fsync → ONLY THEN unlink superseded segments.\n94\tentries()/count() derive the set from the MANIFEST, never a directory glob (glob double-counts during the\n95\tunlink window). Manifest swap + head swap must be crash-consistent together (a crash between them yields a\n96\tfalse TAMPERING window). Seal-time torn-tail truncate on the sealed segment. Carry seq/entry_hash across\n97\tthe seam so verify_chain's contiguity+linkage never break.\n98\t\n99\t## The 5 questions the DESIGN must answer\n100\t1. Where does the durable, non-replayable anti-rollback floor live? (C1) — only relevant if PRUNING.\n101\t2. Exact SignedChainHead schema migration + version-negotiation (old mobile clients / sigil verify vs a v2 head)?\n102\t3. The atomic multi-file commit protocol + which bounded critical section holds the lock (D1/D2/D3)?\n103\t4. Which record classes are un-prunable state-bearers and how is each folded into the signed snapshot? (only if pruning)\n104\t5. Honest contract for resolving a pruned seq (positive \"compacted below N, see snapshot X\" vs a bare 404)?\n105\t\n106\t## Key strategic observation (for the panel to validate or challenge)\n107\tRetain-all SEGMENT ROTATION needs NONE of {C1 durable floor, snapshot state-folding, head-schema prune\n108\tsemantics} — it keeps entry_count absolute, the chain genesis-rooted, all 34 consumers transparent (via a\n109\tsegment-spanning read API), and reclaims practical disk via gzip-on-seal (JSONL ~5-10x). It touches only\n110\tstorage mechanics (manifest, lockfile, epoch-index, cutover) — the HARD-but-BOUNDED engineering. HARD-PRUNE\n111\t(space reclaim by deleting records) is where C1 + state-folding + a breaking head migration live — a\n112\ttamper-evidence tradeoff that is an OPERATOR decision, not an autonomous one.\n113\t"}]},"uuid":"357bfffc-7c95-409f-9e85-d74bbbad1828","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:21:44.261Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"be4381eb-8210-40f7-993f-c1bca2f5c197","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Returns None ONLY when the\n257\t        active is the GENESIS segment (first_seq == 0) — a genuinely fresh spine correctly starts at seq 0.\n258\t        For a rotated (first_seq > 0) empty active whose seam boundary cannot be read, it FAILS CLOSED\n259\t        (raises) rather than returning None — because append treats None as 'genesis' and would otherwise\n260\t        fork the chain at seq 0 (invariant 13). The WRITE path must fail closed exactly like the read path.\n261\t        MUST be consulted under the append lock (reads the manifest fresh). No-op in Slices 0-2 (genesis).\"\"\"\n262\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n263\t        if m is None:\n264\t            return None\n265\t        act = m.active()\n266\t        if act is None or act.first_seq == 0:\n267\t            return None                              # genesis: a fresh spine correctly starts at seq 0\n268\t        sealed = m.sealed_in_order()\n269\t        if sealed:\n270\t            sp = self._layout.seg_path(sealed[-1])\n271\t            line = _last_nonempty_line(sp) if sp.exists() else None\n272\t            if line:\n273\t                d = json.loads(line)\n274\t                return ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"], cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"],\n275\t                                  entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n276\t        raise SpineError(\n277\t            f\"active segment first_seq={act.first_seq} is non-genesis but its seam boundary (the prior \"\n278\t            f\"sealed segment) is unreadable — refusing to fork the chain at seq 0\")\n279\t\n280\t    @contextmanager\n281\t    def _crossproc_lock(self) -> \"Iterator[None]\":\n282\t        \"\"\"Cross-process exclusion on the inode-stable lockfile (invariant 14 / D3). flock binds to the\n283\t        open file DESCRIPTION, not the path, so an os.replace of the manifest/segments cannot move the\n284\t        lock out from under a concurrent writer — which the prior flock-on-the-data-fd could not survive.\n285\t        Best-effort exactly as before (fcntl absent on non-POSIX; a flock failure is swallowed). NOT\n286\t        re-entrant across fds — never nest a second `_crossproc_lock` inside one (a second LOCK_EX on the\n287\t        same file from a different fd self-deadlocks); the seal-swap that runs inside append reuses the\n288\t        already-held lock rather than re-acquiring.\"\"\"\n289\t        if fcntl is None:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n290\t            yield\n291\t            return\n292\t        self._layout.lockfile_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n293\t        fd = os.open(str(self._layout.lockfile_path), os.O_RDWR | os.O_CREAT, 0o600)\n294\t        try:\n295\t            try:\n296\t                fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n297\t            except OSError:  # pragma: no cover\n298\t                pass\n299\t            yield\n300\t        finally:\n301\t            os.close(fd)\n302\t\n303\t    def _complete_orphan_migration_locked(self) -> bool:\n304\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`, and be called only when the manifest is currently ABSENT. If we are\n305\t        in the migrate() crash window — seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone (the rename is atomic + durable),\n306\t        no manifest — finish the interrupted migration by publishing the manifest. Returns True iff it\n307\t        completed one. Called from BOTH __init__'s reconciler AND append's `_refresh_active_under_lock`, so\n308\t        a LIVE appender completes the orphan under the lock instead of re-creating `spine.jsonl` and forking\n309\t        the log (the critical review finding).\"\"\"\n310\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n311\t        if seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n312\t            write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n313\t            _log.warning(\"spine: completed an interrupted migration — published the manifest for %s\", seg0)\n314\t            return True\n315\t        return False\n316\t\n317\t    def _refresh_active_under_lock(self) -> None:\n318\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`. FIRST complete any interrupted migration (so a live appender never\n319\t        resurrects `spine.jsonl`), THEN re-resolve the active segment from the manifest so an appender\n320\t        constructed before a concurrent migrate()/rotation writes to the CURRENT target — never a stale\n321\t        path (fork) or a renamed-away file (resurrection). One manifest read in the steady state.\"\"\"\n322\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n323\t        if m is None and self._complete_orphan_migration_locked():\n324\t            m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n325\t        new_active = self.path\n326\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n327\t        if act is not None:\n328\t            new_active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n329\t        changed = new_active != self._active or (m is not None) != (self._manifest is not None)\n330\t        self._manifest = m                          # keep fresh so the rotation check reuses it (no 2nd read)\n331\t        if changed:\n332\t            self._active = new_active\n333\t            self._invalidate_index()\n334\t            self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n335\t\n336\t    def _invalidate_index(self) -> None:\n337\t        \"\"\"Drops the segment-aware index so the next read rebuilds it against the (possibly changed)\n338\t        segment set.\"\"\"\n339\t        with self._index_lock:\n340\t            self._offsets = {}\n341\t            self._seg_scanned = {}\n342\t            self._index_epoch = None\n343\t            self._max_seq = -1\n344\t\n345\t    def _reconcile_orphan_migration(self) -> None:\n346\t        \"\"\"Close the migrate() crash window at CONSTRUCTION. migrate() does an ATOMIC\n347\t        `os.replace(spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` and THEN publishes the manifest; a crash\n348\t        BETWEEN them leaves seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone, and no manifest — at which point the legacy\n349\t        fallback (active = spine.jsonl) would read the spine as EMPTY and silently hide all history. If we\n350\t        observe exactly that state, finish the (already-durable) migration under the lock. (A live appender\n351\t        does the same via `_refresh_active_under_lock`; a construction-time pass just recovers faster.)\"\"\"\n352\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n353\t            return\n354\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n355\t        if not (seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists()):\n356\t            return\n357\t        with self._crossproc_lock():\n358\t            if read_manifest(self._layout) is None:          # re-check under the lock\n359\t                self._complete_orphan_migration_locked()\n360\t            self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n361\t\n362\t    def change_token(self) -> tuple:\n363\t        \"\"\"A cheap, rotation-aware epoch token — (manifest generation, active file, size, inode) — that\n364\t        changes on ANY append (size), a migration/rotation (generation + active path/inode), or a reset.\n365\t        THE unified replacement for the size-only 'has the spine changed?' heuristic at every site\n366\t        (invariant 9 / A4) — notably the kill-switch verdict caches in `governor/killswitch.py` and\n367\t        `gesture/session.py`. Because it reads the manifest FRESH and keys on the resolved active segment\n368\t        (not the legacy `store.path`, which a migration renames away), a same-size rotation — or a\n369\t        migration performed by another process — can never serve a stale (un-halting) kill-switch verdict.\"\"\"\n370\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n371\t        gen = m.generation if m is not None else -1\n372\t        active = self.path\n373\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n374\t        if act is not None:\n375\t            active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n376\t        try:\n377\t            st = active.stat()\n378\t            return (gen, str(active), st.st_size, st.st_ino)\n379\t        except OSError:\n380\t            return (gen, str(active), -1, -1)\n381\t\n382\t    def migrate(self) -> bool:\n383\t        \"\"\"Move a legacy single-file spine into the segment layout: rename `spine.jsonl` →\n384\t        `<stem>.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl` (atomic, same filesystem — O(1), no byte copy) and publish a\n385\t        single-active-segment manifest. Idempotent (a no-op once a manifest exists). Returns True iff it\n386\t        migrated. SPLIT-BRAIN SAFETY: run this only after every spine writer is on code that flocks the\n387\t        path-stable lockfile (this Slice-0 code) and re-resolves the active segment under that lock — a\n388\t        stale appender then completes/observes the migration instead of resurrecting `spine.jsonl`. After\n389\t        migration `spine.jsonl` is absent and is NEVER re-created as a data file (all data I/O targets the\n390\t        active segment).\"\"\"\n391\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n392\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n393\t                if read_manifest(self._layout) is not None:\n394\t                    self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n395\t                    return False\n396\t                self._layout.segments_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n397\t                seg0_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n398\t                if self.path.exists():\n399\t                    os.replace(self.path, seg0_abs)         # atomic rename (same fs); O(1), no copy\n400\t                    fsync_dir(self._layout.spine_dir)       # persist the SOURCE-dir unlink of spine.jsonl\n401\t                else:\n402\t                    seg0_abs.touch()                        # fresh spine: create the empty active segment\n403\t                fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)        # persist the TARGET-dir creation of seg-0\n404\t                write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n405\t                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n406\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n407\t                self._invalidate_index()\n408\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n409\t                return True\n410\t\n411\t    def reset(self) -> None:\n412\t        \"\"\"Delete EVERY spine artifact for this store — the legacy data file, the manifest, all segments,\n413\t        the trash, and the lockfile — for `sigil ingest --reset` (a full rebuild). Under the cross-process\n414\t        lock so it can't race an append. Idempotent; leaves the store readable (empty) afterward. Replaces\n415\t        the old `SPINE_PATH.unlink()`, which left the manifest + rotated segments behind (stale).\"\"\"\n416\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n417\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n418\t                # NB: do NOT unlink the lockfile — we hold its flock. flock binds to the INODE, not the\n419\t                # path (invariant 14), so unlinking it would let a concurrent cross-process appender open a\n420\t                # NEW inode at the same path and acquire the flock during our destructive rmtree — losing an\n421\t                # acked record and dangling the (re-published) manifest at a deleted segment. It is a tiny\n422\t                # 0-byte token whose entire purpose is path-stability; keep it so reset() truly excludes\n423\t                # appenders for its whole critical section.\n424\t                for pth in (self._layout.manifest_path, self.path):\n425\t                    try:\n426\t                        pth.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n427\t                    except OSError:\n428\t                        pass\n429\t                for dpath in (self._layout.segments_dir, self._layout.trash_dir):\n430\t                    if dpath.exists():\n431\t                        shutil.rmtree(dpath, ignore_errors=True)\n432\t                self._manifest = None\n433\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n434\t                self._invalidate_index()\n435\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n436\t\n437\t    def generation(self) -> int:\n438\t        \"\"\"The manifest generation (monotonic rotation epoch); -1 for a legacy (un-migrated) spine.\"\"\"\n439\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n440\t        return m.generation if m is not None else -1\n441\t\n442\t    def segment_info(self) -> list[dict]:\n443\t        \"\"\"A CLI/human summary of the segment set: one dict per segment in seq order. Empty list for a\n444\t        legacy (un-migrated) single-file spine.\"\"\"\n445\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n446\t        if m is None:\n447\t            return []\n448\t        out: list[dict] = []\n449\t        for seg in m.ordered():\n450\t            p = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n451\t            out.append({\"id\": seg.id, \"codec\": seg.codec, \"sealed\": seg.sealed,\n452\t                        \"first_seq\": seg.first_seq, \"last_seq\": seg.last_seq,\n453\t                        \"bytes\": p.stat().st_size if p.exists() else 0, \"file\": seg.file})\n454\t        return out\n455\t\n456\t    # --- rotation (retain-all seal-swap) ------------------------------------------\n457\t    def _maybe_rotate_locked(self) -> None:\n458\t        \"\"\"MUST hold the flock (called at the end of append, after the ack). Seal the active segment + start\n459\t        a fresh one if it hit a size/record threshold. Only for a MIGRATED store (a legacy single-file spine\n460\t        never auto-rotates — the owner runs `sigil spine migrate` first). Write-then-rotate: the triggering\n461\t        record is already durable in the active before we seal, so a panic append is never blocked by\n462\t        rotation for more than the bounded (O(1)+O(tail)) seal-swap (D1).\"\"\"\n463\t        if not (self._seg_max_bytes or self._seg_max_records):\n464\t            return\n465\t        m = self._manifest                          # kept fresh by _refresh_active_under_lock\n466\t        if m is None:\n467\t            return\n468\t        act = m.active()\n469\t        if act is None:\n470\t            return\n471\t        ap = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n472\t        try:\n473\t            size = ap.stat().st_size\n474\t        except OSError:\n475\t            return\n476\t        tip = self._last.seq if self._last is not None else -1\n477\t        records = (tip - act.first_seq + 1) if tip >= act.first_seq else 0\n478\t        if ((self._seg_max_bytes and size >= self._seg_max_bytes)\n479\t                or (self._seg_max_records and records >= self._seg_max_records)):\n480\t            self._seal_active_locked(m, act, ap)\n481\t\n482\t    def _seal_active_locked(self, m: Manifest, act: Segment, ap: Path) -> bool:\n483\t        \"\"\"MUST hold the flock. Seal the active segment `act` and start a fresh empty active, publishing a\n484\t        new-generation manifest ATOMICALLY (the single commit instant). Retain-all: no record is removed, so\n485\t        the signed head + verify are untouched and entry_count stays absolute. Bounded (O(1)+O(tail)).\n486\t        Ordering is crash-consistent: build the new active durably FIRST, then the manifest swap is the\n487\t        commit — a crash before it leaves the pre-rotation spine intact (the triggering record already\n488\t        durable in the old active); a crash after it leaves the post-rotation set. Returns True if sealed.\"\"\"\n489\t        # R2: seal-time torn-tail truncate (A2). The append-path truncate never revisits a sealed segment,\n490\t        # so this is the ONLY place a just-sealed file is cleaned; usually already clean (we just fsync'd).\n491\t        clean_end, boundary = _last_valid_boundary(ap)\n492\t        if boundary is None:\n493\t            return False                            # empty active — nothing to seal\n494\t        try:\n495\t            pre = ap.stat().st_size\n496\t        except OSError:\n497\t            return False\n498\t        if pre > clean_end:\n499\t            with ap.open(\"r+b\") as f:\n500\t                f.truncate(clean_end)\n501\t                f.flush()\n502\t                os.fsync(f.fileno())\n503\t        seq_hi, boundary_hash = boundary.seq, boundary.entry_hash\n504\t        new_id = act.id + 1\n505\t        new_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(new_id)\n506\t        # R1: GC an orphan next-segment left by a prior CRASHED seal (created but never committed to a\n507\t        # manifest). Safe: it is not in the committed segment set, so it holds no acked record.\n508\t        if new_abs.exists():\n509\t            new_abs.unlink()\n510\t        # R3: create the empty new active durably (it EXISTS before the manifest names it, so a reader that\n511\t        # sees the new manifest always finds the active — no \"active absent\" window).\n512\t        fd = os.open(str(new_abs), os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY, 0o600)\n513\t        os.fsync(fd)\n514\t        os.close(fd)\n515\t        fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)\n516\t        _maybe_crash(\"seal_after_new_active\")       # crash-fuzz: new active created, manifest NOT yet swapped\n517\t        # R4: publish the new-generation manifest — the ATOMIC COMMIT INSTANT (temp→fsync→replace→dir-fsync).\n518\t        sealed = Segment(id=act.id, file=act.file, codec=act.codec, sealed=True,\n519\t                         first_seq=act.first_seq, last_seq=seq_hi, count=seq_hi - act.first_seq + 1,\n520\t                         first_prev_hash=act.first_prev_hash, boundary_hash=boundary_hash, bytes=clean_end)\n521\t        new_active = Segment(id=new_id, file=self._layout.segment_rel(new_id), codec=\"none\", sealed=False,\n522\t                             first_seq=seq_hi + 1, first_prev_hash=boundary_hash)\n523\t        others = [s for s in m.segments if s.id != act.id]\n524\t        write_manifest(self._layout, Manifest(generation=m.generation + 1, scope=m.scope,\n525\t                                              segments=others + [sealed, new_active]))\n526\t        _maybe_crash(\"seal_after_manifest\")         # crash-fuzz: committed; in-memory state not yet updated\n527\t        self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n528\t        self._active = self._layout.seg_path(new_active)\n529\t        self._invalidate_index()\n530\t        self._last = boundary                       # tip preserved; the next append seeds from the seam\n531\t        return True\n532\t\n533\t    def rotate(self) -> bool:\n534\t        \"\"\"Force a seal of the active segment now (for `sigil spine rotate` / an opportunistic seal at\n535\t        checkpoint). No-op on a legacy (un-migrated) or empty active. Returns True if it sealed.\"\"\"\n536\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n537\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n538\t                self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n539\t                m = self._manifest\n540\t                if m is None:\n541\t                    return False\n542\t                act = m.active()\n543\t                if act is None:\n544\t                    return False\n545\t                return self._seal_active_locked(m, act, self._layout.seg_path(act))\n546\t\n547\t    # --- write --------------------------------------------------------------------\n548\t    def append(\n549\t        self, *, kind: str, source: str, actor: str, payload: dict[str, Any],\n550\t        parent_id: int | None = None, supersedes_id: int | None = None,\n551\t        ts: str | None = None,\n552\t    ) -> int:\n553\t        content = {\n554\t            \"scope\": SCOPE, \"kind\": kind, \"source\": source, \"actor\": actor,\n555\t            \"payload\": payload, \"parent_id\": parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": supersedes_id,\n556\t        }\n557\t        cert_digest = digest_payload(content)  # wallclock-free\n558\t        # Serialize the whole read-tip → write so concurrent writers (threaded bridge server, gesture\n559\t        # daemon) can't both fork off a stale tip and break the chain. The in-process RLock is keyed on\n560\t        # the STABLE `self.path` (unchanged), so the check-then-append gate `envelope.consume` builds on\n561\t        # top of it stays atomic across a rotation. Re-read the TRUE tip from disk under the lock.\n562\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n563\t            with self._crossproc_lock():                 # cross-process guard on the path-stable lockfile\n564\t                self._refresh_active_under_lock()        # pick up a concurrent migrate()/rotation; never fork\n565\t                active = self._active\n566\t                # binary append+read: lets us TRUNCATE a torn tail before writing (BLOCK-1 fix). `a+b`\n567\t                # creates the file if absent and — in append mode — every write still lands at EOF.\n568\t                with active.open(\"a+b\") as f:\n569\t                    # BLOCK-1: an interrupted write can leave torn bytes PAST the last valid record. Appending\n570\t                    # after them would MERGE (torn + new) into one unparseable line → the new record is silently\n571\t                    # lost, verify() stays green, and a lost kill-switch panic never halts the mesh. Truncate the\n572\t                    # dead tail back to the last valid record FIRST (this only removes never-committed garbage\n573\t                    # from an interrupted write; committed records are untouched). Evaluated on the ACTIVE\n574\t                    # segment under the lock (invariant 7 — the append target is always the true chain tip).\n575\t                    clean_end, last = _last_valid_boundary(active)\n576\t                    pre_size = os.fstat(f.fileno()).st_size\n577\t                    if pre_size > clean_end:\n578\t                        f.truncate(clean_end)\n579\t                        _log.warning(\"spine: truncated a %d-byte torn tail (interrupted write) before append (%s)\",\n580\t                                     pre_size - clean_end, active)\n581\t                    if last is not None:\n582\t                        entry = append_entry([last], cert_digest)          # extend the active segment's tip\n583\t                    else:\n584\t                        # active is EMPTY: seed from the prior sealed segment's boundary if this is a rotated\n585\t                        # (non-genesis) segment, else start the genesis chain at seq 0. Never build_chain a\n586\t                        # non-genesis segment from GENESIS (that would fork the chain at seq 0 — invariant 13).\n587\t                        seam = self._seam_tip_for_active()\n588\t                        entry = append_entry([seam], cert_digest) if seam is not None else build_chain([cert_digest])[0]\n589\t                    record = {\n590\t                        \"seq\": entry.seq, **content, \"ts\": ts or now_iso(),\n591\t                        \"cert_digest\": cert_digest, \"prev_hash\": entry.prev_hash, \"entry_hash\": entry.entry_hash,\n592\t                    }\n593\t                    line = json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\"\n594\t                    offset = clean_end                      # after any truncate, EOF == clean_end (where the line lands)\n595\t                    f.write(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n596\t                    f.flush()\n597\t                    os.fsync(f.fileno())                    # FIX 3: an ack'd append is durable across a crash\n598\t                    _maybe_crash(\"append_after_fsync\")      # crash-fuzz: record durable, not yet acked/sealed\n599\t                    # FIX 1: keep the index current with NO re-scan when the ACTIVE segment is already\n600\t                    # indexed exactly up to this record's offset. If another PROCESS appended in between,\n601\t                    # `_seg_scanned[active] < offset` and we skip — the next read's `_ensure_index` extends\n602\t                    # the active over the gap bytes AND this one. `_index_epoch` is intentionally left stale\n603\t                    # so that next read refreshes it (its extend then finds nothing to do — O(1)).\n604\t                    with self._index_lock:\n605\t                        active_str = str(active)\n606\t                        if self._seg_scanned.get(active_str) == offset:\n607\t                            self._offsets[entry.seq] = (active_str, offset)\n608\t                            if entry.seq > self._max_seq:\n609\t                                self._max_seq = entry.seq\n610\t                            self._seg_scanned[active_str] = offset + len(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n611\t                self._last = entry\n612\t                # write-then-rotate: the record is durable (fsync'd) above BEFORE any seal, so a panic\n613\t                # append is never blocked by rotation for more than the bounded seal-swap (D1). No-op unless\n614\t                # a migrated active hit a threshold.\n615\t                self._maybe_rotate_locked()\n616\t        return entry.seq\n617\t\n618\t    # --- read ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n619\t    def _open_segment(self, p: Path):\n620\t        \"\"\"Open a segment for a forward byte read. Plaintext in Slices 1-3; gzip-transparent in Slice 4.\"\"\"\n621\t        return p.open(\"rb\")\n622\t\n623\t    def iter_records(self, *, since_seq: int = -1) -> Iterator[SpineRecord]:\n624\t        \"\"\"Records with seq > `since_seq`, in seq order, ACROSS ALL SEGMENTS (sealed segments then the\n625\t        active) as ONE contiguous chain, so every full-scanning consumer keeps seeing the whole log. For\n626\t        since_seq >= 0 the index seeks straight to the first wanted line in its owning segment\n627\t        (O(records-returned), not O(spine)); a full read (since_seq < 0) streams every segment from the\n628\t        first. A line that fails to parse or lacks required keys is SKIPPED (a torn active tail can't crash\n629\t        the read; a torn MIDDLE line becomes a seq gap that verify() fails on). RAISES SpineError if the\n630\t        manifest references a segment that cannot be read — a read must NEVER silently yield a short chain\n631\t        (invariant 15: no state-scanner fail-open).\"\"\"\n632\t        segs = self._segments_in_order()                 # RAISES on a missing referenced segment\n633\t        if not segs:\n634\t            return\n635\t        start_i, start_off = self._start_locus(since_seq, segs)\n636\t        for i in range(start_i, len(segs)):\n637\t            p = segs[i]\n638\t            try:\n639\t                f = self._open_segment(p)\n640\t            except FileNotFoundError as e:               # referenced but vanished mid-read\n641\t                raise SpineError(f\"spine segment vanished during read: {p}\") from e\n642\t            with f:\n643\t                if i == start_i and start_off:\n644\t                    f.seek(start_off)\n645\t                for raw in f:\n646\t                    if not raw.strip():\n647\t                        continue\n648\t                    try:\n649\t                        rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n650\t                    except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n651\t                        _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed line during iter_records (%s)\", p)\n652\t                        continue\n653\t                    if rec.seq > since_seq:\n654\t                        yield rec\n655\t\n656\t    def get(self, seq: int) -> SpineRecord | None:\n657\t        \"\"\"A single record by seq — O(1) via the segment-aware index (resolves the OWNING segment + offset),\n658\t        byte-identical to a full scan. Falls back to a bounded forward scan (which spans segments) when the\n659\t        seq is not indexed (beyond the tip, or a corrupt line was skipped).\"\"\"\n660\t        if seq < 0:\n661\t            return None\n662\t        self._ensure_index()\n663\t        with self._index_lock:\n664\t            loc = self._offsets.get(seq)\n665\t        if loc is not None:\n666\t            path_str, off = loc\n667\t            try:\n668\t                with self._open_segment(Path(path_str)) as f:\n669\t                    f.seek(off)\n670\t                    raw = f.readline()\n671\t                rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n672\t                if rec.seq == seq:\n673\t                    return rec\n674\t            except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n675\t                pass                                     # stale/moved offset — fall through to the scan\n676\t        for r in self.iter_records(since_seq=seq - 1):\n677\t            if r.seq == seq:\n678\t                return r\n679\t            if r.seq > seq:\n680\t                break\n681\t        return None\n682\t\n683\t    def tail(self, n: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n684\t        \"\"\"The last `n` records, walking segments backward from the ACTIVE across the seam (O(n) bytes,\n685\t        NOT O(spine)) — a bounded RECENT-window read on a large spine. Fewer than `n` if the spine is\n686\t        shorter. FIXES B3: the pre-Slice-1 version read only the active file, so right after a rotation it\n687\t        returned < n. NOTE (unchanged): a bounded window collapses a rapid recent replay flood but does NOT\n688\t        bound AGGREGATE replay bloat — pair `tail()`-based dedup with a record-time freshness gate.\"\"\"\n689\t        if n <= 0:\n690\t            return []\n691\t        recs: list[SpineRecord] = []\n692\t        for p in reversed(self._segments_in_order()):\n693\t            recs = self._read_segment_tail(p, n - len(recs)) + recs\n694\t            if len(recs) >= n:\n695\t                break\n696\t        return recs[-n:]\n697\t\n698\t    def _read_segment_tail(self, p: Path, k: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n699\t        \"\"\"The last `k` records of a single segment (seek-from-end, O(k) bytes). Plaintext in Slices 1-3.\"\"\"\n700\t        if k <= 0 or not p.exists():\n701\t            return []\n702\t        with p.open(\"rb\") as f:\n703\t            f.seek(0, os.SEEK_END)\n704\t            pos = f.tell()\n705\t            buf = b\"\"\n706\t            while pos > 0 and buf.count(b\"\\n\") <= k:\n707\t                step = min(65536, pos)\n708\t                pos -= step\n709\t                f.seek(pos)\n710\t                buf = f.read(step) + buf\n711\t        out: list[SpineRecord] = []\n712\t        for ln in [x for x in buf.split(b\"\\n\") if x.strip()][-k:]:\n713\t            try:\n714\t                out.append(SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(ln)))\n715\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n716\t                continue\n717\t        return out\n718\t\n719\t    @property\n720\t    def next_seq(self) -> int:\n721\t        return (self._last.seq + 1) if self._last else 0\n722\t\n723\t    def count(self) -> int:\n724\t        return sum(1 for _ in self.iter_records())\n725\t\n726\t    # --- integrity ----------------------------------------------------------------\n727\t    def entries(self) -> list[ChainEntry]:\n728\t        return [\n729\t            ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash)\n730\t            for r in self.iter_records()\n731\t        ]\n732\t\n733\t    def verify(self) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n734\t        \"\"\"Two-layer UNKEYED integrity: (1) BINDING — each record's payload still hashes to its\n735\t        stored cert_digest (catches silent payload edits); (2) CHAIN — the entries link cleanly\n736\t        (catches delete/reorder/entry tamper). This proves internal CONSISTENCY, not authenticity:\n737\t        a naive payload edit fails (1), and a mid-chain digest edit cascades an entry_hash/prev_hash\n738\t        break caught by (2) — BUT a writer who recomputes cert_digest+entry_hash for the tip (no\n739\t        successor to cascade into) or forward-cascades a fork produces a self-consistent chain that\n740\t        passes here. Resistance to a recompute-capable writer is the owner-SIGNED head's job\n741\t        (`checkpoint.verify_checkpoint`, Ed25519 + monotonic last_seq). Use this for corruption/\n742\t        naive-tamper detection; use the signed head for tamper-EVIDENCE.\"\"\"\n743\t        entries: list[ChainEntry] = []\n744\t        for r in self.iter_records():\n745\t            content = {\n746\t                \"scope\": r.scope, \"kind\": r.kind, \"source\": r.source, \"actor\": r.actor,\n747\t                \"payload\": r.payload, \"parent_id\": r.parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": r.supersedes_id,\n748\t            }\n749\t            if digest_payload(content) != r.cert_digest:\n750\t                return False, f\"binding break at seq {r.seq}: payload does not match cert_digest (record tampered)\"\n751\t            entries.append(ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash))\n752\t        return verify_chain(entries)\n753\t\n754\t    @staticmethod\n755\t    def _entry_from_line(line: str | None) -> \"ChainEntry | None\":\n756\t        if not line:\n757\t            return None\n758\t        d = json.loads(line)\n759\t        return ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"], cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"],\n760\t                          entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n761\t\n762\t    def _read_last_entry(self) -> ChainEntry | None:\n763\t        \"\"\"The chain tip = the last valid record of the ACTIVE segment. If the active is EMPTY (a freshly\n764\t        rotated segment before its first append), the tip is the last SEALED segment's real last record\n765\t        (the seam boundary), so next_seq never regresses / reuses a seq across a rotation (invariant 13).\n766\t        `_last_nonempty_line` skips a torn/garbage tail, so a crash mid-write can't block a restart.\"\"\"\n767\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n768\t        if m is None:\n769\t            t = self._read_target()\n770\t            return self._entry_from_line(_last_nonempty_line(t) if t.exists() else None)\n771\t        act = m.active()\n772\t        if act is not None:\n773\t            ap = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n774\t            line = _last_nonempty_line(ap) if ap.exists() else None\n775\t            if line:\n776\t                return self._entry_from_line(line)\n777\t        for seg in reversed(m.sealed_in_order()):        # active empty/absent -> last sealed segment's tail\n778\t            sp = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n779\t            line = _last_nonempty_line(sp) if sp.exists() else None\n780\t            if line:\n781\t                return self._entry_from_line(line)\n782\t        return None\n783\t\n784\t    # --- segment-aware seq -> (segment, byte-offset) index ------------------------\n785\t    def _start_locus(self, since_seq: int, segs: list[Path]) -> tuple[int, int]:\n786\t        \"\"\"(starting segment index in `segs`, byte offset within it) so a forward read yields exactly\n787\t        seq > since_seq. A full read (since_seq < 0) → (0, 0). Otherwise the index seeks to seq\n788\t        (since_seq + 1) in its owning segment; if that seq is beyond the tip, return a past-the-end index\n789\t        so nothing is yielded; if not resolvable (below the min, or a skipped/corrupt gap), fall back to a\n790\t        safe full span from segment 0.\"\"\"\n791\t        if since_seq < 0:\n792\t            return (0, 0)\n793\t        self._ensure_index()\n794\t        want = since_seq + 1\n795\t        with self._index_lock:\n796\t            loc = self._offsets.get(want)\n797\t            max_seq = self._max_seq\n798\t        if loc is not None:\n799\t            path_str, off = loc\n800\t            for i, p in enumerate(segs):\n801\t                if str(p) == path_str:\n802\t                    return (i, off)\n803\t            return (0, 0)                           # segment set changed under us → safe full span\n804\t        if want > max_seq:\n805\t            return (len(segs), 0)                   # nothing beyond the tip → yield nothing\n806\t        return (0, 0)                               # below min / gap → safe full span\n807\t\n808\t    def _ensure_index(self) -> None:\n809\t        \"\"\"Build/reconcile the segment-aware index lazily, keyed on the epoch (`change_token`). On an epoch\n810\t        change (an append grew the active, or a rotation/migration changed the segment set) reconcile each\n811\t        segment: the ACTIVE extends over the new bytes (or re-scans if it shrank in place — a truncation);\n812\t        a SEALED segment is immutable and indexed exactly once. Thread-safe under `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n813\t        tok = self.change_token()\n814\t        segs = self._segments_in_order()            # RAISES on a missing referenced segment, so get() (which\n815\t        #                                             calls _ensure_index) fails CLOSED too — never a\n816\t        #                                             fail-OPEN point read from a surviving segment while the\n817\t        #                                             chain is provably truncated (invariant 15).\n818\t        active_str = str(segs[-1]) if segs else None\n819\t        with self._index_lock:\n820\t            if tok == self._index_epoch:\n821\t                return\n822\t            for p in segs:\n823\t                ps = str(p)\n824\t                try:\n825\t                    size = p.stat().st_size\n826\t                except OSError:\n827\t                    continue\n828\t                scanned = self._seg_scanned.get(ps, 0)\n829\t                if ps == active_str:\n830\t                    if size > scanned:\n831\t                        self._scan_segment(ps, p, scanned, size)     # extend the active over new bytes\n832\t                    elif size < scanned:                             # active shrank in place (truncation)\n833\t                        self._purge_segment(ps)\n834\t                        self._scan_segment(ps, p, 0, size)\n835\t                elif scanned < size:                                 # sealed: immutable → index once\n836\t                    self._scan_segment(ps, p, scanned, size)\n837\t            self._index_epoch = tok\n838\t\n839\t    def _purge_segment(self, ps: str) -> None:\n840\t        \"\"\"Drop a segment's offsets (the active shrank in place — a truncation). MUST hold `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n841\t        self._offsets = {s: loc for s, loc in self._offsets.items() if loc[0] != ps}\n842\t        self._seg_scanned.pop(ps, None)\n843\t        self._max_seq = max(self._offsets, default=-1)\n844\t\n845\t    def _scan_segment(self, ps: str, p: Path, start: int, size: int) -> None:\n846\t        \"\"\"Index every COMPLETE (newline-terminated) record line in bytes [start, size) of segment `p` as\n847\t        `_offsets[seq] = (ps, line_offset)`. MUST hold `_index_lock`. A malformed complete line is left OUT\n848\t        (so a mid-file gap still surfaces via verify()); a trailing partial line is left for the next\n849\t        extend.\"\"\"\n850\t        if size <= start:\n851\t            return\n852\t        with p.open(\"rb\") as f:\n853\t            f.seek(start)\n854\t            chunk = f.read(size - start)\n855\t        consumed = 0\n856\t        while True:\n857\t            nl = chunk.find(b\"\\n\", consumed)\n858\t            if nl == -1:\n859\t                break                                       # trailing partial line — not complete yet\n860\t            raw = chunk[consumed:nl]\n861\t            line_off = start + consumed\n862\t            consumed = nl + 1\n863\t            if not raw.strip():\n864\t                continue\n865\t            try:\n866\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n867\t                if not all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n868\t                    raise KeyError\n869\t                seq = d[\"seq\"]\n870\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n871\t                continue                                    # corrupt complete line — do not index it\n872\t            self._offsets[seq] = (ps, line_off)\n873\t            if seq > self._max_seq:\n874\t                self._max_seq = seq\n875\t        self._seg_scanned[ps] = start + consumed\n876\t"}]},"uuid":"109d830b-b4c8-43dd-a664-7e8b4fece1e2","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:21:57.254Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e723e414-de02-40c2-8642-c0a1e348404d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"e4b7a244-a4eb-4df9-aff8-804d590bc960","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"aec4ab0b760cd2873","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VpLWCuzBSbx1fqHpTE5pUh","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"The spine's segment MANIFEST — the sole linearization point for the retain-all segment set.\n2\t\n3\tThe spine is stored as a sequence of size-bounded, immutable **sealed** segments plus exactly one\n4\t**active** (append) segment; the manifest names them in seq order. It is deliberately UNSIGNED and\n5\tNON-LOAD-BEARING: record order, count, and every tamper decision are re-derived from the segment BYTES\n6\t(via `verify_chain`/`classify_head`), never from the manifest's convenience fields. A doctored manifest\n7\ttherefore fails CLOSED — dropping/reordering an interior segment breaks `prev_hash`/seq contiguity, a\n8\tdropped tail shortens `entries()` (the signed head's `n < entry_count` gate fires), a dropped front fails\n9\tthe genesis check. (A future signed-manifest tier would set `manifest_sig` — reserved `null` — because it\n10\twould then attest pruned ranges; retain-all needs no such trust.)\n11\t\n12\t`SpineLayout` derives the whole on-disk layout from the spine data-file path a `SpineStore` is constructed\n13\twith, so a store on a temp path is fully isolated. This module does no locking — the caller (`SpineStore`)\n14\tholds the cross-process flock while reading/writing/migrating.\n15\t\"\"\"\n16\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n17\t\n18\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n19\tfrom pathlib import PurePosixPath, Path\n20\t\n21\tfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, field_validator\n22\t\n23\tfrom ..reuse.chain import _GENESIS_PREV\n24\tfrom .atomicio import atomic_write_text\n25\t\n26\tSEGMENT_STEM = \"seg-\"\n27\t_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1\n28\t\n29\t\n30\tdef segment_filename(seg_id: int, codec: str = \"none\") -> str:\n31\t    \"\"\"Zero-padded segment file name; `.gz` suffix when gzip-sealed (Slice 4).\"\"\"\n32\t    base = f\"{SEGMENT_STEM}{seg_id:08d}.jsonl\"\n33\t    return base + \".gz\" if codec == \"gzip\" else base\n34\t\n35\t\n36\tclass Segment(BaseModel):\n37\t    \"\"\"One segment in the manifest. `first_prev_hash`/`boundary_hash` are the chain-linkage the reader\n38\t    CROSS-CHECKS against the segment's real first/last records — they are convenience, not authority.\"\"\"\n39\t    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")   # forward-compatible: tolerate fields a newer writer added\n40\t    id: int = Field(ge=0)\n41\t    file: str                                    # path RELATIVE to the spine dir, e.g. \"segments/seg-00000000.jsonl\"\n42\t    codec: str = \"none\"                          # \"none\" | \"gzip\"\n43\t    sealed: bool = False\n44\t    first_seq: int = Field(ge=0)\n45\t    last_seq: int | None = None                  # null while active\n46\t    count: int | None = None                     # null while active; CONVENIENCE ONLY — never a tamper input\n47\t    first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV         # == the prior segment's boundary_hash (seg 0 == genesis)\n48\t    boundary_hash: str | None = None             # == entry_hash(last_seq); null while active\n49\t    bytes: int = 0\n50\t    sha256: str | None = None                    # at-rest file integrity; NOT a chain digest\n51\t\n52\t    @field_validator(\"file\")\n53\t    @classmethod\n54\t    def _file_is_contained_relative(cls, v: str) -> str:\n55\t        \"\"\"A segment `file` must be a plain relative path under the spine dir — never absolute and never\n56\t        `..`-escaping. `get()`/`iter_records()`/`tail()` open segment files WITHOUT running verify(), so a\n57\t        doctored manifest pointing `file` at `/etc/passwd` or `../../secret` would otherwise surface that\n58\t        file's bytes AS spine records. Reject it at the model boundary so the read path stays fail-closed\n59\t        even though the manifest itself is unsigned.\"\"\"\n60\t        pp = PurePosixPath(v)\n61\t        if not v or pp.is_absolute() or PurePosixPath(v.replace(\"\\\\\", \"/\")).is_absolute() or \"..\" in pp.parts:\n62\t            raise ValueError(f\"segment file must be a contained relative path, got {v!r}\")\n63\t        return v\n64\t\n65\t\n66\tclass Manifest(BaseModel):\n67\t    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")\n68\t    schema_version: int = _SCHEMA_VERSION\n69\t    generation: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)     # monotonic epoch; ++ on EVERY swap — the unified change token\n70\t    scope: str = \"\"\n71\t    segments: list[Segment] = Field(default_factory=list)\n72\t    manifest_sig: str | None = None              # reserved for the deferred signed-manifest tier\n73\t\n74\t    def active(self) -> Segment | None:\n75\t        \"\"\"The single unsealed (append) segment, if any. There is at most one.\"\"\"\n76\t        act = [s for s in self.segments if not s.sealed]\n77\t        return act[-1] if act else None\n78\t\n79\t    def sealed_in_order(self) -> list[Segment]:\n80\t        return sorted((s for s in self.segments if s.sealed), key=lambda s: s.first_seq)\n81\t\n82\t    def ordered(self) -> list[Segment]:\n83\t        \"\"\"All segments in seq order (sealed by first_seq, then the active tail).\"\"\"\n84\t        out = self.sealed_in_order()\n85\t        act = self.active()\n86\t        if act is not None:\n87\t            out.append(act)\n88\t        return out\n89\t\n90\t\n91\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n92\tclass SpineLayout:\n93\t    \"\"\"The on-disk layout derived from a spine data-file path (…/spine.jsonl). Every artifact is\n94\t    NAMESPACED BY THE DATA FILE'S STEM (`spine.manifest.json`, `spine.lock`, `spine.segments/`) so two\n95\t    stores whose data files merely SHARE a directory — e.g. the `tempfile.mktemp()` idiom that drops files\n96\t    straight into /tmp — never collide on a manifest/lock/segments set. Without this, one migrated store's\n97\t    `manifest.json` would be read by every other store in that directory and silently steer it onto the\n98\t    wrong segment (a real cross-store contamination the review caught).\"\"\"\n99\t    data_path: Path            # the legacy/identity path (…/spine.jsonl) — the stable lock/identity token\n100\t    spine_dir: Path\n101\t    segments_dir: Path\n102\t    manifest_path: Path\n103\t    lockfile_path: Path\n104\t    trash_dir: Path\n105\t\n106\t    @classmethod\n107\t    def for_path(cls, data_path: Path | str) -> \"SpineLayout\":\n108\t        p = Path(data_path)\n109\t        d = p.parent\n110\t        ns = p.stem or p.name       # \"spine.jsonl\" -> \"spine\"; namespaces every derived artifact\n111\t        return cls(\n112\t            data_path=p, spine_dir=d, segments_dir=d / f\"{ns}.segments\",\n113\t            manifest_path=d / f\"{ns}.manifest.json\", lockfile_path=d / f\"{ns}.lock\", trash_dir=d / f\"{ns}.trash\",\n114\t        )\n115\t\n116\t    def seg_path(self, seg: Segment) -> Path:\n117\t        \"\"\"Absolute path of a segment's file (its `file` is stored relative to the spine dir). The\n118\t        `Segment.file` validator guarantees it is a plain relative path that cannot escape spine_dir.\"\"\"\n119\t        return self.spine_dir / seg.file\n120\t\n121\t    def segment_rel(self, seg_id: int, codec: str = \"none\") -> str:\n122\t        \"\"\"The manifest `file` value (relative to spine_dir) for a segment id — under the namespaced\n123\t        segments dir, e.g. 'spine.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl'.\"\"\"\n124\t        return f\"{self.segments_dir.name}/{segment_filename(seg_id, codec)}\"\n125\t\n126\t\n127\tdef read_manifest(layout: SpineLayout) -> Manifest | None:\n128\t    \"\"\"Load the manifest, or None if absent. A present-but-unparseable manifest RAISES (fail closed) —\n129\t    the atomic write path guarantees it is never torn, so a parse failure means genuine corruption, not a\n130\t    partial write, and must never be silently treated as 'no manifest' (which would strand the segments).\"\"\"\n131\t    mp = layout.manifest_path\n132\t    if not mp.exists():\n133\t        return None\n134\t    return Manifest.model_validate_json(mp.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n135\t\n136\t\n137\tdef write_manifest(layout: SpineLayout, manifest: Manifest) -> None:\n138\t    \"\"\"Atomically publish the manifest (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). THE cutover commit instant.\"\"\"\n139\t    layout.manifest_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n140\t    atomic_write_text(layout.manifest_path, manifest.model_dump_json(), prefix=\".manifest-\")\n141\t\n142\t\n143\tdef initial_manifest(scope: str, *, active_file: str, first_seq: int = 0,\n144\t                     first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV) -> Manifest:\n145\t    \"\"\"A single-active-segment manifest (generation 0) — the shape produced by a fresh store or a\n146\t    migration of an existing single-file spine.\"\"\"\n147\t    return Manifest(\n148\t        generation=0, scope=scope,\n149\t        segments=[Segment(id=0, file=active_file, codec=\"none\", sealed=False,\n150\t                          first_seq=first_seq, first_prev_hash=first_prev_hash)],\n151\t    )\n152\t"}]},"uuid":"59503bf1-3962-4daf-807f-d9a8ec79a9c6","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:22:02.426Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e4b7a244-a4eb-4df9-aff8-804d590bc960","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"757e0825-835b-4fde-ab51-55d7cc74bdf1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"aec4ab0b760cd2873","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EYZk2JcFjzBrZCt3zmSgqF","type":"tool_result","content":"atomicio.py\ncheckpoint.py\n__init__.py\nmanifest.py\nmodels.py\nstore.py\ntail.py\nverify.py\n---ATOMICIO---\n\"\"\"Durable, atomic file replacement — the one implementation the spine's crash-safe swaps share.\n\nExtracted verbatim from `checkpoint._atomic_write_text` (FIX 3) so the signed head, the manifest, and\nany future single-file cutover all use ONE audited routine rather than drifting copies. The contract:\na reader observes either the whole old file or the whole new file (never a torn one), and the new\ncontent survives a crash at any point — a crash mid-write leaves the PREVIOUS valid file intact.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n\ndef fsync_dir(directory: Path | str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"fsync a directory so a rename/create inside it is itself durable across a crash. A no-op-on-error\n    on filesystems that don't support directory fsync (the rename still lands; only its durability window\n    widens).\"\"\"\n    try:\n        dfd = os.open(str(directory), os.O_DIRECTORY)\n        try:\n            os.fsync(dfd)\n        finally:\n            os.close(dfd)\n    except OSError:  # pragma: no cover — dir fsync unsupported on some filesystems\n        pass\n\n\ndef atomic_write_text(path: Path | str, data: str, *, prefix: str = \".tmp-\") -> None:\n    \"\"\"Durably + atomically replace `path` with `data`: write a temp file in the SAME dir, fsync it,\n    `os.replace()` over the target (atomic on POSIX), then fsync the directory so the rename survives a\n    crash. A crash at any point leaves the previous valid file intact, never a partially-written one.\"\"\"\n    path = Path(path)\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=str(path.parent), prefix=prefix, suffix=\".tmp\")\n    try:\n        with os.fdopen(fd, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n            f.write(data)\n            f.flush()\n            os.fsync(f.fileno())\n        os.replace(tmp, path)\n    except BaseException:\n        try:\n            os.unlink(tmp)\n        except OSError:\n            pass\n        raise\n    fsync_dir(path.parent)","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"bfa16932-ca89-4d44-83d6-fbaf820df6d4","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:22:06.209Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"757e0825-835b-4fde-ab51-55d7cc74bdf1","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"a5cc6fa2-d508-4df6-bbd8-2ad8200c9f9d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"aec4ab0b760cd2873","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QC5JGq142fgbiD7dbxiKE3","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Daily signed checkpoint of the spine head (SIGIL §6.3.6, WARDEN anchor).\n2\t\n3\tGenerates + persists a solo-owner Ed25519 keypair once (1-of-1 trust root), then signs\n4\tthe chain head so the whole spine is anchored: rewriting history would require forging\n5\tthe owner signature, and a shrunk/back-dated head is caught by the monotonic last_seq.\n6\t\n7\tNote: for a personal, single-owner, local-first system the owner's signing key lives\n8\tlocally (0600) — a deliberate simplification of the framework's \"keys never on the\n9\truntime host\" rule, appropriate to this threat model (§1.3). A hardware co-signer can be\n10\tadded later by raising the trust-root threshold.\n11\t\"\"\"\n12\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n13\t\n14\timport os\n15\tfrom pathlib import Path\n16\t\n17\tfrom ..config import HEAD_PATH, KEYS_DIR, OWNER_KEY_ID, SCOPE\n18\tfrom ..reuse import (\n19\t    AuthorizerKey,\n20\t    SignedChainHead,\n21\t    TrustRoot,\n22\t    generate_keypair,\n23\t    sign_head,\n24\t    verify_head,\n25\t)\n26\tfrom .atomicio import atomic_write_text\n27\tfrom .store import SpineStore\n28\t\n29\t_PRIV = KEYS_DIR / \"owner.priv\"\n30\t_PUB = KEYS_DIR / \"owner.pub\"\n31\t\n32\t\n33\tdef _atomic_write_text(path: Path, data: str) -> None:\n34\t    \"\"\"Durably + atomically replace `path` with `data` (FIX 3) — a reader sees either the whole old or\n35\t    the whole new head, never a torn one, and a crash leaves the previous valid head intact. Delegates to\n36\t    the one shared `spine.atomicio.atomic_write_text` so the head, the manifest, and every cutover use the\n37\t    same audited routine (no drift).\"\"\"\n38\t    atomic_write_text(path, data, prefix=\".head-\")\n39\t\n40\t\n41\tdef _owner_keys() -> tuple[str, str]:\n42\t    if not _PRIV.exists():\n43\t        KEYS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n44\t        kp = generate_keypair()\n45\t        _PRIV.write_text(kp.private_key_b64, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n46\t        os.chmod(_PRIV, 0o600)\n47\t        _PUB.write_text(kp.public_key_b64, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n48\t    return _PRIV.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").strip(), _PUB.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").strip()\n49\t\n50\t\n51\tdef trust_root() -> TrustRoot:\n52\t    _, pub = _owner_keys()\n53\t    return TrustRoot(threshold=1, authorizers=[\n54\t        AuthorizerKey(key_id=OWNER_KEY_ID, name=\"owner\", public_key_b64=pub)])\n55\t\n56\t\n57\tdef checkpoint(store: SpineStore | None = None) -> SignedChainHead:\n58\t    store = store or SpineStore()\n59\t    priv, _ = _owner_keys()\n60\t    head = sign_head(store.entries(), engagement_slug=SCOPE, signers=[(OWNER_KEY_ID, priv)])\n61\t    _atomic_write_text(HEAD_PATH, head.model_dump_json())   # FIX 3: never leave a torn head on a crash\n62\t    return head\n63\t\n64\t\n65\tdef classify_head(head: SignedChainHead, entries: list, tr: TrustRoot) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n66\t    \"\"\"Pure head/chain classification (no globals — testable in isolation). Distinguishes\n67\t    three cases honestly: a shrunk chain (truncation) or a mismatching prefix (history\n68\t    rewrite) are TAMPERING; a chain that merely grew past the anchor is benign — stale.\n69\t    \"\"\"\n70\t    n, signed_n = len(entries), head.entry_count\n71\t    if n < signed_n:\n72\t        return False, f\"TAMPERING: chain has {n} records but the signed head anchors {signed_n} (truncated/rolled back)\"\n73\t    # verify the head against exactly the prefix it signed; append-only ⇒ the prefix is byte-identical.\n74\t    ok, msg = verify_head(head, entries[:signed_n], tr)\n75\t    if not ok:\n76\t        return False, f\"TAMPERING: history rewritten at or below the signed head — {msg}\"\n77\t    if n > signed_n:\n78\t        return True, f\"anchors {signed_n} records; {n - signed_n} appended since — run `sigil sign` to re-anchor\"\n79\t    return True, f\"anchors all {signed_n} records (current)\"\n80\t\n81\t\n82\tdef verify_checkpoint(store: SpineStore | None = None) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n83\t    store = store or SpineStore()\n84\t    if not HEAD_PATH.exists():\n85\t        return False, \"no signed head — run `sigil sign` to anchor the spine\"\n86\t    head = SignedChainHead.model_validate_json(HEAD_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n87\t    return classify_head(head, store.entries(), trust_root())\n88\t"}]},"uuid":"b3135ce9-5732-42b7-83d1-d0858a764a14","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:22:13.128Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a5cc6fa2-d508-4df6-bbd8-2ad8200c9f9d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
